# Database Schema (SQLite, schema version 8)

This document describes the local SQLite database used by the desktop app. There is no backend in this repository.

## Conventions

- **Money:** stored as `INTEGER` minor units (e.g., `1250` = 12.50 Br). Never floating point.
- **Billing quantities:** stored as `INTEGER` milliunits (e.g., `2000` = 2.000) where a numeric quantity is meaningful; `NULL` for free-text/manual-amount lines.
- **IDs:** UUID strings generated on the device.
- **Snapshots:** `sale_items` stores the product name and price at sale time; it never joins to the current product price.
- **Timestamps:** ISO-8601 strings (`DateTime.toIso8601String()`), compared as strings for ranges and sorted descending for history.
- **Foreign keys:** enabled with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` on every connection.

## Tables

### `users`
- `id` TEXT PK
- `username` TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL (case-insensitive login)
- `password_hash` TEXT NOT NULL (`pbkdf2-sha256$v=1$i=<iterations>$<base64>`)
- `salt` TEXT NOT NULL (random per user)
- `role` TEXT NOT NULL (`OWNER` or `CASHIER`)
- `is_active` INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 1
- `created_at` TEXT NOT NULL

### `products`
- `id` TEXT PK, `name`, `code` (UNIQUE), `category`, `unit`
- `selling_price_minor_units` INTEGER
- `is_active` INTEGER, `created_at`, `updated_at`

### `devices`
- `id` TEXT PK � stable per installation, persisted in `device_id.txt` next to the database
- `name`, `platform`, `license_key`, `last_sync_at`

### `shifts`
- `id` TEXT PK, `cashier_id` TEXT
- `opening_cash_minor_units`, `closing_cash_minor_units`, `expected_cash_minor_units`
- `status` TEXT (`OPEN`/`CLOSED`), `opened_at`, `closed_at`

A partial unique index (`one_open_shift`) plus insert/update triggers enforce at most one open shift.

### `cash_movements`
- `id` TEXT PK, `shift_id` TEXT FK -> shifts, `user_id` TEXT FK -> users
- `type` TEXT (`DEPOSIT`, `WITHDRAWAL`, `EXPENSE`), `amount_minor_units`, `reason`, `created_at`

### `sales`
- `id` TEXT PK, `receipt_number` TEXT UNIQUE (`REC-YYYYMMDD-NNNN`, sequential per day)
- `cashier_id` FK -> users, `device_id` FK -> devices, `shift_id` FK -> shifts
- `customer_name`, `payment_method` (`CASH`/`BANK`)
- `subtotal_minor_units`, `discount_minor_units`, `total_minor_units`
- `amount_paid_minor_units`, `change_minor_units`
- `status` (`COMPLETED`/`VOIDED`), `void_reason`, `voided_at`, `voided_by`, `voided_shift_id`
- `created_at`, `synced_at`

Voids are soft deletes. `voided_shift_id` attributes the cash refund to the shift that was open when the void happened; refunds fall back to the original `shift_id` for legacy voids.

### `void_requests` (cashier-initiated, owner-approved voids)
- `id` TEXT PK
- `sale_id` TEXT FK -> sales
- `requested_by` TEXT FK -> users (the cashier who flagged the sale)
- `reason` TEXT NOT NULL
- `status` TEXT NOT NULL (`PENDING`/`APPROVED`/`REJECTED`, default `PENDING`)
- `created_at` TEXT NOT NULL
- `decided_by` TEXT FK -> users (nullable � set when the owner approves/rejects)
- `decided_at` TEXT (nullable)

A pending request leaves the sale `COMPLETED` and does not touch revenue or the cash drawer. Only an active `OWNER` may approve (which runs the normal void path, marking the sale `VOIDED` and stamping `voided_shift_id`) or reject. A direct owner void auto-settles any `PENDING` request on that sale.

### `sale_items`
- `id` TEXT PK, `sale_id` FK -> sales ON DELETE CASCADE
- `product_id` TEXT (catalog ID, or `MANUAL` for free-form items)
- `product_name_snapshot`, `unit_price_snapshot_minor_units`
- `quantity_text` TEXT NOT NULL � the operator's exact wording (e.g. `"2 boxes and 1 piece"), never parsed for billing
- `billing_quantity_milliunits` INTEGER NULL � the numeric quantity actually charged (milliunits); `NULL` when the line is billed by a manual amount instead of a quantity
- `line_discount_minor_units`, `line_total_minor_units`
- `notes` TEXT � free-text remark on the line

### `finance_categories` (owner-only money tracking)
- `id` TEXT PK, `name` TEXT NOT NULL (a person/source label)
- `created_at`, `updated_at`

### `finance_entries` (owner-only money tracking)
- `id` TEXT PK, `category_id` TEXT FK -> finance_categories
- `source` TEXT NOT NULL, `amount_minor_units` INTEGER NOT NULL (> 0)
- `description` TEXT
- `status` TEXT (`PENDING`/`COMPLETED`), `entry_date` TEXT
- `created_at`, `created_by` FK -> users, `completed_at`, `completed_by` FK -> users, `updated_at`

Finance entries are completely separate from sales: completing one never affects revenue. Owner-only access is enforced in the data layer (every read/write runs inside a transaction that re-checks the acting user is an active `OWNER`).

### `audit_logs`
- `id` TEXT PK, `user_id` TEXT (nullable for unknown login attempts)
- `action` TEXT (e.g. `LOGIN_SUCCEEDED`, `LOGIN_FAILED`, `SALE_COMPLETED`, `SALE_VOIDED`, `SHIFT_OPENED`, `SHIFT_CLOSED`, `CASH_MOVEMENT_ADDED`, `PASSWORD_CHANGED`, `PASSWORD_RESET`, `USER_CREATED`, `USER_ACTIVATED`, `USER_DEACTIVATED`, `BACKUP_EXPORTED`, `BACKUP_RESTORED`, and `FINANCE_*` for money-tracking mutations)
- `details` TEXT, `created_at` TEXT

### `counters`
- `name` TEXT PK (e.g. `receipt-20261021`), `value` INTEGER � sequential receipt allocation.

### `settings`
- `key` TEXT PK, `value` TEXT � currently stores `theme_mode` (`light`/`dark`).

## Indexes

- `one_open_shift` (partial unique on `shifts.status WHERE status = 'OPEN'`)
- `sales_shift_id`, `sales_created_at`
- `sale_items_sale_id`
- `audit_logs_created_at`
- `cash_movements_shift_id`
- `finance_entries_category_id`
- `void_requests_sale_id`, `void_requests_status`

## Migrations

`LocalDatabase.schemaVersion` is the single source of truth (currently `8`). Both `onCreate` and `onUpgrade` build the same final shape:

- v2: shifts
- v3: `sales.payment_method`
- v4: `users.salt`, `sales.shift_id`, `sales.void_reason`, `cash_movements`
- v5: void attribution columns, `cash_movements.user_id`, open-shift index/triggers, shift index
- v6: rebuilds `cash_movements` with a real `user_id` foreign key, adds `counters`/`settings`, and creates all indexes/triggers on upgraded databases
- v7: rebuilds `sale_items` to replace the integer `quantity` with `quantity_text` (the old number is preserved as text), `billing_quantity_milliunits` (old quantity x 1000), and `notes`; adds `finance_categories`/`finance_entries` and the `finance_entries_category_id` index
- v8: adds the `void_requests` table with `void_requests_sale_id` and `void_requests_status` indexes

## Backups

`DataExporter` is owner-only and writes a JSON document at the current schema version containing every table, including `void_requests` and password hashes. `DataImporter` is owner-only, validates required fields and owner retention, writes a recovery snapshot first, replaces all rows in one transaction, and runs `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` before commit. Backups at schema versions 6 through 8 are accepted; v6 rows are normalized to the flexible-quantity shape (integer quantities become `quantity_text`/`billing_quantity_milliunits`), and `finance_categories`/`finance_entries`/`void_requests` are optional in older backups and simply restore empty. Versions older than 6 are rejected rather than guessed at. After a restore the app re-registers the current device row so new sales keep valid foreign keys.

## Register cash math

Expected cash for an open shift is `opening + cash_sales + deposits - withdrawals - cross_shift_cash_refunds`. `cash_sales` counts only `COMPLETED` cash sales, so a sale rung up and voided in the **same** shift is a pure no-op (it never entered cash sales, and must not be subtracted again). Only voids of a cash sale whose `shift_id` differs from the currently open `voided_shift_id` genuinely reduce the drawer. `getShiftSummary` and `closeShift` use this identical rule so the on-screen figure and the stored `expected_cash_minor_units` always agree.

FILE: docs\LICENSING.md
==================================================

# License signing and key rotation

Licenses use `base64url(canonical-json).base64url(Ed25519-signature)`. Canonical JSON is UTF-8 compact JSON with every object key recursively sorted; arrays retain order. Version 1 payloads require `format`, `version`, `product`, `entitlements`, `issued_at`, and `expires_at`. `hardware_id` is optional; when present it is checked against the machine fingerprint (Windows WMI UUID, falling back to the hostname).

The app never embeds the private key. The public verification key is supplied at build time:

```powershell
flutter build windows --release `
  --dart-define=WHOLESALE_POS_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY=<64-hex-char-public-key>
```

When the key is absent, licensing fails closed and the activation screen explains how to configure it. The former HMAC license format is rejected; the old HMAC secret is compromised. Existing HMAC licenses must be reissued.

## Operator setup

From `app/`, run:

```powershell
dart run tools/setup_keys.dart <private-key-path-outside-project>
```

The tool refuses to write inside the repository or overwrite an existing key. It writes the private seed outside the project and prints the public key plus the exact build command. Keep the private key in an access-controlled, backed-up location and never commit it. The public key is not secret, but it is injected at build time instead of being edited into source so multiple builds can target different keys.

If the printed public key is lost, re-derive it from the escrowed seed:

```powershell
dart run tools/print_public_key.dart <private-key-path> [license-key-path]
```

The tool prints the public key and, when a license path is supplied, verifies that the license signature matches the key.

Issue a license with:

```powershell
dart run tools/generate_license.dart <hardware-id-or-> <private-key-path> <output-license-path>
```

Use `-` only for an intentionally unbound license. The tool refuses to overwrite an existing output. Place the resulting file next to the executable (or in the working directory) as `license.key` for the customer. `flutter build windows` replaces the executable but leaves extra files in the release folder alone, so copy `license.key` into `build\windows\x64\runner\Release\` after each clean build and include it in any distribution archive.

## Development without a key

To run the app locally before a production key exists:

```powershell
flutter run -d windows --dart-define=WHOLESALE_POS_DEV_UNLICENSED=true
```

This escape hatch is compiled in only when the define is explicitly set and no public key is configured. It must never be used for a distributed build. `flutter test` does not need it; tests inject a verification key directly.

## Rotation

Generate and securely escrow a new private key, rebuild with the new `WHOLESALE_POS_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY`, distribute the signed application update, and reissue every license. The current verifier trusts one public key, so old licenses stop validating when the new build ships; plan an orderly transition. Removing `app/license.key` from the latest Git revision does not remove it or the old HMAC secret from public Git history. History rewriting and coordination with every clone/fork owner require a separate operator-led incident response.

## Limitations

- The license file is a signed document on disk, not a server-verified entitlement. A machine administrator can delete or restore it, but cannot forge a valid signature.
- The verifier rejects licenses whose `issued_at` is more than one day in the future (a coarse clock-rollback check). It does not persist the last-seen time, so a clock set backwards within the license validity window is not detected.

# Project Progress

## Completed

- **Phase 1**: Requirements and feasibility; Windows/Flutter toolchain verified.
- **Phase 2, Milestone 1**: Flutter shell, project baseline, Git.
- **Phase 2, Milestone 2**: SQLite schema v6, migrations, indexes, triggers, and backup/restore. Upgraded to schema v7 (see Phase 7).
- **Phase 3**: Core POS flow � login, product catalog, cart, cash/bank checkout, sequential receipts, PDF receipts, sales history.
- **Phase 4**: Shift register � opening/closing cash, cash movements, expected-cash reconciliation, void verification.
- **Phase 5**: Administration � owner dashboard, reports, user management, audit log, theme persistence, owner-only export.
- **Phase 6**: Security hardening � standard PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 with per-user salts and background isolate, legacy hash migration, password change/reset, account deactivation, Ed25519 licensing with build-time public key.
- **Phase 7**: Wholesale cash-register upgrade (schema v6 ? v7):
  - Manual sales entry: free-typed item names, manual prices, and free-text quantities (e.g. "2 boxes and 1 piece") kept strictly separate from the billed amount; manual line amounts supported. Text is never silently parsed into a number.
  - Exact integer money (minor units) and billing quantities (milliunits) with round-half-up line totals and overflow checks.
  - Ethiopian Birr (Br) formatting throughout.
  - Optional customer name on sales.
  - Redesigned sales history: no receipt numbers shown, searchable by product/customer/cashier/notes, filterable by today/yesterday/this week/this month/this year/custom ranges.
  - Dashboard and reports labeled "revenue" (never profit), voided sales excluded, midnight reset via date filtering, period filters on summaries.
  - Owner-only private money tracking (people/sources, pending/completed lifecycle) enforced in the data layer, wired into the app shell and navigation.
  - Product catalog repurposed into optional "Saved Names" (quick-pick names with an optional default price; add/edit/deactivate/restore).
  - Backup/restore extended to the finance tables and to accepting v6 backups (normalized to v7).
- **Phase 8**: Register math, void-request workflow, and UI refinement (schema v7 ? v8):
  - Fixed the expected-cash double count: a sale rung up and voided in the same shift now nets to zero, and only cross-shift cash refunds reduce the drawer. `getShiftSummary` and `closeShift` share the identical rule, and the register shows a transparent `opening + cash sales + deposits - withdrawals - cash refunds = expected cash` breakdown.
  - Cashier mistake workflow: a cashier can flag any completed sale as a **void request** (with a reason); the sale stays completed until an owner **approves** (which voids it) or **rejects** it, from the register's "Pending Void Requests" card or the sale detail. Enforced owner-only in the data layer; a direct owner void auto-settles pending requests.
  - DineFlow-style recent sales: a "Recent Sales" (today + yesterday) panel on the POS screen and a compact, date-grouped Sales History with tight single-line rows and void/request badges.
  - Dashboard upgrade: dependency-free custom charts (revenue-trend bars, cash/bank payment split, top-products bars) plus comparison KPIs (revenue vs the previous period, void count, pending void requests, average per sale) in a tighter responsive grid.
  - Backup/restore extended to `void_requests`; versions 6 through 8 accepted.
- **Phase 9**: Register (cash-drawer) history:
  - A searchable **Register History** screen (opened from the owner's register app bar) lists every past and current shift newest-first, filterable by day/week/month/year/custom range and by open/closed status.
  - Each row shows who ran it, the time window, and the counted-vs-expected discrepancy; tapping reveals the full drawer breakdown (opening, cash sales, deposits, withdrawals, cross-shift refunds, expected, counted, discrepancy) plus bank sales and attributed voids.
  - Closed shifts keep their stored expected/counted figures, so historical reconciliation is never rewritten by later voids; new `ShiftRepository.getShiftHistory` and a display-only `cashierName`/`discrepancy` on the `Shift` model (no schema change).

## Verification

- `flutter analyze`: clean.
- `flutter test`: 113 tests passing (database, v5?v6/v6?v7/v7?v8 migrations, repositories incl. sale search/dashboard/revenue-trend/voids and the void-request lifecycle, corrected register cash math, register/shift history filtering and discrepancy, owner-only finance, money/quantity/date-range parsing, backup import/normalization incl. void_requests, cart, widget).
- `flutter build windows --release`: succeeds (built with the licensing `--dart-define=WHOLESALE_POS_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY=<hex>` flag; `license.key` shipped next to the executable).

## Next Up

- Stock/inventory movements and low-stock alerts.
- Purchases, suppliers, and cost tracking.
- Credit sales, customer accounts, and receivables.
- Discounts and tax rules beyond the current zero-discount cart.
- Returns/refunds as a separate document rather than void-only.
- Fractional quantities for kg/litre products.
- Report export (CSV/PDF) and date-ranged summaries.
- Optional backend sync (the `synced_at` columns are placeholders).
- Mobile packaging; the current UI and database factory target Windows desktop.

FILE: README.md
==================================================

# Wholesale POS

A Windows desktop point-of-sale and wholesale management application built with Flutter.

## Features

- Owner/cashier sign-in with PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 password hashing (per-user salt, background isolate) and automatic migration of legacy hashes.
- Product catalog with SKU, unit, category, and price; POS catalog search plus a free-form manual item entry.
- Cart, cash/bank checkout, change calculation, sequential daily receipt numbers, and printable PDF receipts.
- Shift register: opening float, deposits/withdrawals/expenses, expected cash, closing count and discrepancy.
- Sales history with date/receipt filters, receipt details, and owner-only void with a required reason.
- Owner dashboard and reports (top products, cashier performance), user management (create, reset password, activate/deactivate), and audit log.
- Owner-only JSON backup/restore with validation, recovery snapshot, and foreign-key checks.
- Ed25519 licensing with build-time public key injection.

## Requirements

- Windows 10/11
- Flutter 3.47+ with the Windows desktop toolchain (Visual Studio 2022 "Desktop development with C++")
- Dart 3.13+

## Run

```powershell
cd app
flutter pub get
flutter run -d windows --dart-define=WHOLESALE_POS_DEV_UNLICENSED=true
```

The `WHOLESALE_POS_DEV_UNLICENSED` define is a development-only escape hatch; see `docs/LICENSING.md`. Without it, the app requires a valid `license.key` and a build-time public key.

On first launch the app asks you to create the owner account. After signing in, open the register before recording sales.

## Build

```powershell
cd app
flutter build windows --release `
  --dart-define=WHOLESALE_POS_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY=<public-key-hex>
Copy-Item license.key build\windows\x64\runner\Release\license.key
```

The executable is written to `build\windows\x64\runner\Release\wholesale_pos_app.exe`. The build must include the public key, and `license.key` must sit next to the executable (or in the working directory) or the app shows the activation screen.

## Test and analyze

```powershell
cd app
flutter analyze
flutter test
dart format --output=none --set-exit-if-changed lib test tools
```

## Data

- Database: SQLite in the platform application-support directory (`wholesale_pos.db`), schema v6.
- Device identity: `device_id.txt` next to the database.
- Backups: JSON files written to the user's Documents directory; owner-only and include password hashes, so store them securely.
- Schema details: `docs/DATABASE.md`.

## Structure

- `app/` � Flutter application
- `app/tools/` � key setup and license generation
- `docs/` � schema, licensing, and progress documentation

The project currently targets Windows desktop. The UI uses desktop-sized layouts with responsive stacking below roughly 900 px; mobile packaging has not been implemented.
